**[[108]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-108 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed, 2001, c.
25, s. 61\]]{.Repealed}

### [Inquiries]{.HTitleText2} {#h-141574 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inquiry

- **[[109]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-109 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Minister may, for any purpose related to the administration or
  enforcement of this Act, authorize any person to make an inquiry into
  any matter specified by the Minister.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers of person authorized

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A person authorized pursuant to subsection (1) has
  all of the powers of a person appointed as a commissioner under Part I
  of the [Inquiries Act](/eng/acts/I-11).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Travel and living expenses

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Reasonable travel and living expenses shall be paid
  to any person summoned by a person authorized under subsection (1) at
  the time of the service of the summons.

### [Penalties and Interest]{.HTitleText2} {#h-141582 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Designated provisions

- **[[109.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-109.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every person who fails to comply
  with any provision of an Act or a regulation designated by the
  regulations made under subsection (3) is liable to a penalty of not
  more than twenty-five thousand dollars, as the Minister may direct.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Failure to comply

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every person who fails to comply with any term or
  condition of a licence issued under this Act or the [Customs
  Tariff](/eng/acts/C-54.011) or any obligation undertaken under section
  4.1 is liable to a penalty of not more than twenty-five thousand
  dollars, as the Minister may direct.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Designation by regulation

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may make regulations

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} designating any provisions of this Act, the
    [Customs Tariff](/eng/acts/C-54.011) or the [Special Import Measures
    Act](/eng/acts/S-15) or of any regulation made under any of those
    Acts; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} establishing short-form descriptions of the
    provisions designated under paragraph (a) and providing for the use
    of those descriptions.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1993, c. 25, s. 80
- 1995, c. 41, s. 29
- 1997, c. 36, s. 182
- 2001, c. 25, s. 62
:::

**[[109.11]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-109.11 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed,
2001, c. 25, s. 62\]]{.Repealed}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition of [[designated
goods]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

- **[[109.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-109.2
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In this section, [[designated
  goods]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} includes firearms, weapons, ammunition and
  any other goods classified under Chapter 93 of the List of Tariff
  Provisions set out in the schedule to the [Customs
  Tariff](/eng/acts/C-54.011) or under tariff item No. 9898.00.00 of
  that List.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contravention relating to tobacco,
  cannabis and vaping products and to designated goods

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every person that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} removes tobacco products, cannabis products, vaping
    products or designated goods or causes tobacco products, cannabis
    products, vaping products or designated goods to be removed from a
    customs office, sufferance warehouse, bonded warehouse or duty free
    shop in contravention of this Act or the [Customs
    Tariff](/eng/acts/C-54.011) or the regulations made under those
    Acts, or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} sells or uses tobacco products or designated goods
    designated as ships' stores in contravention of this Act or the
    [Customs Tariff](/eng/acts/C-54.011) or the regulations made under
    those Acts,

  is liable to a penalty equal to double the total of the duties that
  would be payable on like tobacco products, cannabis products, vaping
  products or designated goods released in like condition at the rates
  of duties applicable to like tobacco products, cannabis products,
  vaping products or designated goods at the time the penalty is
  assessed, or to such lesser amount as the Minister may direct.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1993, c. 25, s. 80
- 1995, c. 41, s. 29
- 1997, c. 36, s. 184
- 2018, c. 12, s. 116
- [2022, c. 10, s. 88](#1356488-1376927){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1356488-1376927.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-109.2-20181017.html "Link to previous version of section 109.2"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Assessment

- **[[109.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-109.3
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A penalty to which a person is
  liable under section 109.1 or 109.2 may be assessed by an officer and,
  if an assessment is made, an officer shall serve on the person a
  written notice of that assessment by sending it by registered or
  certified mail or delivering it to the person.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation on assessment

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A person shall not be assessed penalties under both
  sections 109.1 and 109.2 in respect of the same contravention of this
  Act, the [Customs Tariff](/eng/acts/C-54.011) or the [Special Import
  Measures Act](/eng/acts/S-15) or the regulations made under those
  Acts.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Penalty in addition to other sanction

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An assessment under subsection (1) may be made in
  addition to a seizure under this Act or a demand for payment under
  section 124, in respect of the same contravention of this Act or the
  regulations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Sufficiency of short-form description

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The use on a notice of assessment of a short-form
  description established under paragraph 109.1(3)(b) or of a
  description that deviates from that description without affecting its
  substance is sufficient for all purposes to describe the
  contravention.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1993, c. 25, s. 80
- 1995, c. 41, s. 30
- 2001, c. 25, s. 63
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}When penalty becomes payable

**[[109.4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-109.4 .sectionLabel}** A penalty assessed
against a person under section 109.3 shall become payable on the day the
notice of assessment of the penalty is served on the person.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1993, c. 25, s. 80
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interest on penalties

- **[[109.5]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-109.5
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), a person
  on whom a notice of assessment of a penalty has been served under
  section 109.3 shall pay, in addition to the penalty, interest at the
  prescribed rate for the period beginning on the day after the notice
  was served on the person and ending on the day the penalty has been
  paid in full, calculated on the outstanding balance of the penalty.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Interest is not payable if the penalty is paid in
  full by the person within thirty days after the date of the notice of
  assessment.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1993, c. 25, s. 80
- 2001, c. 25, s. 64
:::

### [Seizures]{.HTitleText2} {#h-141627 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Seizure of goods or conveyances

- **[[110]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-110 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  officer may, where he believes on reasonable grounds that this Act or
  the regulations have been contravened in respect of goods, seize as
  forfeit

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the goods; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any conveyance that the officer believes on
    reasonable grounds was made use of in respect of the goods, whether
    at or after the time of the contravention.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Seizure of conveyances

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An officer may, where he believes on reasonable
  grounds that this Act or the regulations have been contravened in
  respect of a conveyance or in respect of persons transported by a
  conveyance, seize as forfeit the conveyance.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Seizure of evidence

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An officer may, where he believes on reasonable
  grounds that this Act or the regulations have been contravened, seize
  anything that he believes on reasonable grounds will afford evidence
  in respect of the contravention.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of seizure

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} An officer who seizes goods or a conveyance as
  forfeit under subsection (1) or (2) shall take such measures as are
  reasonable in the circumstances to give notice of the seizure to any
  person who the officer believes on reasonable grounds is entitled to
  make an application under section 138 in respect of the goods or
  conveyance.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Information for search warrant

- **[[111]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-111 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  justice of the peace who is satisfied by information on oath in the
  form set out as Form 1 in Part XXVIII of the [Criminal
  Code](/eng/acts/C-46), varied to suit the case, that there are
  reasonable grounds to believe that there will be found in a building,
  receptacle or place

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any goods or conveyance in respect of which this
    Act or the regulations have been contravened or are suspected of
    having been contravened,

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any conveyance that has been made use of in respect
    of such goods, whether at or after the time of the contravention, or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} anything that there are reasonable grounds to
    believe will afford evidence in respect of a contravention of this
    Act or the regulations,

  may at any time issue a warrant under his hand authorizing an officer
  to search the building, receptacle or place for any such thing and to
  seize it.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Execution in another territorial
  jurisdiction

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A justice of the peace may, where a building,
  receptacle or place referred to in subsection (1) is in a territorial
  division other than that in which the justice of the peace has
  jurisdiction, issue his warrant in a form similar to the form referred
  to in subsection (1), modified according to the circumstances, and the
  warrant may be executed in the other territorial division after it has
  been endorsed, in the manner set out in Form 28 of Part XXVIII of the
  [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46), by a justice of the peace having
  jurisdiction in that territorial division.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Seizure of things not specified

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An officer who executes a warrant issued under
  subsection (1) may seize, in addition to the things mentioned in the
  warrant,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any goods or conveyance in respect of which the
    officer believes on reasonable grounds that this Act or the
    regulations have been contravened;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any conveyance that the officer believes on
    reasonable grounds was made use of in respect of such goods, whether
    at or after the time of the contravention; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} anything that the officer believes on reasonable
    grounds will afford evidence in respect of a contravention of this
    Act or the regulations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Execution of search warrant

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A warrant issued under subsection (1) shall be
  executed by day, unless the justice of the peace, by the warrant,
  authorizes execution of it by night.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Form of search warrant

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} A warrant issued under subsection (1) may be in the
  form set out as Form 5 in Part XXVIII of the [Criminal
  Code](/eng/acts/C-46), varied to suit the case.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Where warrant not necessary

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} An officer may exercise any of the powers referred to
  in subsection (1) without a warrant if the conditions for obtaining
  the warrant exist but by reason of exigent circumstances it would not
  be practical to obtain the warrant.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exigent circumstances

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection (6), exigent
  circumstances include circumstances in which the delay necessary to
  obtain a warrant under subsection (1) would result in danger to human
  life or safety or the loss or destruction of anything liable to
  seizure.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 1 (2nd Supp.), s. 111
- 1992, c. 1, s. 143(E)
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers of entry

**[[112]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-112 .sectionLabel}** For the purpose of
exercising his authority under section 111, an officer may, with such
assistance as he deems necessary, break open any door, window, lock,
fastener, floor, wall, ceiling, compartment, plumbing fixture, box,
container or any other thing.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation for seizures and ascertained
forfeitures

**[[113]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-113 .sectionLabel}** No seizure may be made
under this Act or notice sent under section 124 more than six years
after the contravention or use in respect of which such seizure is made
or notice is sent.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Custody of things seized

- **[[114]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-114
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Anything that is seized under this
  Act shall forthwith be placed in the custody of an officer.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Report where evidence seized

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where an officer seizes anything as evidence under
  this Act, the officer shall forthwith report the circumstances of the
  case to the President.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Return of evidence

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Anything that is seized under this Act as evidence
  alone shall be returned forthwith on completion of all proceedings in
  which the thing seized may be required.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 1 (2nd Supp.), s. 114
- 1999, c. 17, s. 127
- 2005, c. 38, s. 85
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-114-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 114"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Copies of records

- **[[115]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-115 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If
  any record is examined or seized under this Act, the Minister, or the
  officer by whom it is examined or seized, may make or cause to be made
  one or more copies of it, and a copy purporting to be certified by the
  Minister or a person authorized by the Minister is admissible in
  evidence and has the same probative force as the original would have
  if it had been proved in the ordinary way.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Detention of records seized

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} No record that has been seized as evidence under this
  Act shall be detained for a period of more than three months unless,
  before the expiration of that period,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the person from whom it was seized agrees to its
    further detention for a specified period;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a justice of the peace is satisfied on application
    that, having regard to the circumstances, its further detention for
    a specified period is warranted and he or she so orders; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} judicial proceedings are instituted in which the
    seized record may be required.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 1 (2nd Supp.), s. 115
- 2001, c. 25, s. 65
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Goods stopped or taken by peace officer

**[[116]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-116 .sectionLabel}** Where a peace officer
detains or seizes anything that he suspects is subject to seizure under
this Act, he shall forthwith notify an officer thereof and describe the
thing detained or seized to the officer.

### [Return of Goods Seized]{.HTitleText2} {#h-141688 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Return of goods seized

- **[[117]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-117 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  officer may, subject to this or any other Act of Parliament, return
  any goods that have been seized under this Act to the person from whom
  they were seized or to any person authorized by the person from whom
  they were seized on receipt of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an amount of money of a value equal to

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the aggregate of the value for duty of the goods
      and the amount of duties levied thereon, if any, calculated at the
      rates applicable thereto

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} at the time of seizure, if the goods have not
        been accounted for under subsection 32(1), (2) or (5) or if
        duties or additional duties have become due on the goods under
        paragraph 32.2(2)(b) in circumstances to which subsection
        32.2(6) applies, or

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} at the time the goods were accounted for under
        subsection 32(1), (2) or (5), in any other case, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} such lesser amount as the Minister may direct;
      or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} where the Minister so authorizes, security
    satisfactory to the Minister.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No return of certain goods

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (1), if spirits, wine, specially
  denatured alcohol, restricted formulations, cannabis, raw leaf
  tobacco, excise stamps, tobacco products or vaping products are seized
  under this Act, they shall not be returned to the person from whom
  they were seized or any other person unless they were seized in error.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 1 (2nd Supp.), s. 117
- 1995, c. 41, s. 31
- 1997, c. 36, s. 185
- 2002, c. 22, s. 338
- 2007, c. 18, s. 137
- 2010, c. 12, s. 50
- 2018, c. 16, s. 170
- [2022, c. 10, s. 89](#1356490-1376930){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1356490-1376930.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-117-20181017.html "Link to previous version of section 117"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Return of conveyance seized

**[[118]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-118 .sectionLabel}** An officer may, subject
to this or any other Act of Parliament, return any conveyance that has
been seized under this Act to the person from whom it was seized or to
any person authorized by the person from whom it was seized on receipt
of

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} an amount of money of a value equal to

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the value of the conveyance at the time of seizure,
    as determined by the Minister, or

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} such lesser amount as the Minister may direct; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} where the Minister so authorizes, security
  satisfactory to the Minister.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Return of animals or perishable goods
seized

- **[[119]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-119 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  officer shall, subject to this or any other Act of Parliament, return
  any animals or perishable goods that have been seized under this Act
  and have not been sold under subsection (2) to the person from whom
  they were seized or to any person authorized by the person from whom
  they were seized at the request of such person and on receipt of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an amount of money of a value equal to

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the aggregate of the value for duty of the
      animals or perishable goods and the amount of duties levied
      thereon, if any, calculated at the rates applicable thereto,

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} at the time of seizure, if the animals or
        perishable goods have not been accounted for under subsection
        32(1), (2) or (5) or if duties or additional duties have become
        due on the goods under paragraph 32.2(2)(b) in circumstances to
        which subsection 32.2(6) applies, or

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} at the time the animals or perishable goods
        were accounted for under subsection 32(1), (2) or (5), in any
        other case, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} such lesser amount as the Minister may direct;
      or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} where the Minister so authorizes, security
    satisfactory to the Minister.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Sale of seized goods

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An officer may sell any animals or perishable goods
  that have been seized under this Act, in order to avoid the expense of
  keeping them or to avoid their deterioration, at any time after giving
  the person from whom they were seized or the owner thereof a
  reasonable opportunity to obtain the animals or perishable goods under
  subsection (1), and the proceeds of the sale shall be held as forfeit
  in lieu of the thing sold.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 1 (2nd Supp.), s. 119
- 1995, c. 41, s. 32
- 1997, c. 36, s. 186
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Dealing with goods seized

- **[[119.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-119.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where any goods are seized under
  this Act, the Minister may authorize an officer to sell, destroy or
  otherwise deal with the goods.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Excise stamps not to be sold

  [(1.01)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (1), the Minister shall not
  authorize an officer to sell excise stamps that have been seized under
  this Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Restriction

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to the regulations, the sale under
  subsection (1) of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} spirits or specially denatured alcohol may only be
    to a spirits licensee;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} wine may only be to a wine licensee;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} raw leaf tobacco or a tobacco product may only be
    to a tobacco licensee;

  - [(c.1)]{.lawlabel} a vaping product may only be to a vaping product
    licensee; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a restricted formulation may only be to a licensed
    user.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Proceeds of sale

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister shall hold the proceeds from the sale of
  any goods under subsection (1) as forfeit in lieu of the goods sold.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Payment of compensation

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where a person would be entitled to the return of
  goods if they were available to be returned, but it is not possible to
  return them, the person shall be paid

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} where the goods were sold, the proceeds from the
    sale; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in any other case, the value of the goods.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1994, c. 37, s. 9
- 2002, c. 22, s. 339
- 2007, c. 18, s. 138
- 2010, c. 12, s. 51
- [2022, c. 10, s. 90](#1356492-1376933){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1356492-1376933.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-119.1-20100712.html "Link to previous version of section 119.1"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Value substituted for value for duty

**[[120]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-120 .sectionLabel}** For the purpose of
calculating the amount of money referred to in paragraph 117(a) or
119(1)(a), where the value for duty of goods cannot be ascertained, the
value of the goods at the time of seizure, as determined by the
Minister, may be substituted for the value for duty thereof.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Goods no longer forfeit

**[[121]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-121 .sectionLabel}** Goods or conveyances in
respect of which money or security is received under section 117, 118 or
119 shall cease to be forfeit from the time the money or security is
received and the money or security shall be held as forfeit in lieu
thereof.

### [Forfeitures]{.HTitleText2} {#h-141744 .Subheading}

#### [General]{.HTitleText3} {#h-141745 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Forfeitures accrue automatically from
time of contravention

**[[122]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-122 .sectionLabel}** Subject to the reviews
and appeals established by this Act, any goods or conveyances that are
seized as forfeit under this Act within the time period set out in
section 113 are forfeit

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} from the time of the contravention of this Act or the
  regulations in respect of which the goods or conveyances were seized,
  or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a conveyance made use of in respect of
  goods in respect of which this Act or the regulations have been
  contravened, from the time of such use,

and no act or proceeding subsequent to the contravention or use is
necessary to effect the forfeiture of such goods or conveyances.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Review of forfeiture

**[[123]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-123 .sectionLabel}** The forfeiture of goods
or conveyances seized under this Act or any money or security held as
forfeit in lieu of such goods or conveyances is final and not subject to
review or to be restrained, prohibited, removed, set aside or otherwise
dealt with except to the extent and in the manner provided by sections
127.1 and 129.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 1 (2nd Supp.), s. 123
- 2001, c. 25, s. 66
:::

#### [Ascertained Forfeiture]{.HTitleText3} {#h-141755 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Ascertained forfeitures

- **[[124]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-124
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where an officer believes on
  reasonable grounds that a person has contravened any of the provisions
  of this Act or the regulations in respect of any goods or conveyance,
  the officer may, if the goods or conveyance is not found or if the
  seizure thereof would be impractical, serve a written notice on that
  person demanding payment of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an amount of money determined under subsection (2)
    or (3), as the case may be; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} such lesser amount as the Minister may direct.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Determination of amount of payment in
  respect of goods

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of paragraph (1)(a), an officer may
  demand payment in respect of goods of an amount of money of a value
  equal to the aggregate of the value for duty of the goods and the
  amount of duties levied thereon, if any, calculated at the rates
  applicable thereto

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} at the time the notice is served, if the goods have
    not been accounted for under subsection 32(1), (2) or (5) or if
    duties or additional duties have become due on the goods under
    paragraph 32.2(2)(b) in circumstances to which subsection 32.2(6)
    applies; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} at the time the goods were accounted for under
    subsection 32(1), (2) or (5), in any other case.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Determination of amount of payment in
  respect of conveyances

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of paragraph (1)(a), an officer may
  demand payment in respect of a conveyance of an amount of money of a
  value equal to the value of the conveyance at the time the notice is
  served, as determined by the Minister.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Value substituted for value for duty

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of calculating the amount of money
  referred to in subsection (2), where the value for duty of goods
  cannot be ascertained, the value of the goods at the time the notice
  is served under subsection (1), as determined by the Minister, may be
  substituted for the value for duty thereof.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Value of exported goods

  [(4.1)]{.lawlabel} Sections 117 and 119 and subsection (2) apply to a
  contravention of this Act or the regulations in respect of goods that
  have been or are about to be exported, except that the references to
  "value for duty of the goods" in those provisions are to be read as
  references to "value of the goods".

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Value of goods

  [(4.2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection (4.1), the
  expression [[value of the goods]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the total
  of all payments made or to be made by the purchaser of the goods to or
  for the benefit of the vendor.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Value of goods set by Minister

  [(4.3)]{.lawlabel} If the value of the goods cannot be determined
  under subsection (4.2), the Minister may determine that value.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Service of notice

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Service of the notice referred to in subsection (1)
  is sufficient if it is sent by registered mail addressed to the person
  on whom it is to be served at his latest known address.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interest

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} A person on whom a notice of ascertained forfeiture
  has been served shall pay, in addition to the amount set out in the
  notice, interest at the prescribed rate for the period beginning on
  the day after the notice was served and ending on the day the amount
  is paid in full, calculated on the outstanding balance. However,
  interest is not payable if the amount is paid in full within thirty
  days after the date of the notice.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 1 (2nd Supp.), s. 124
- 1995, c. 41, s. 33
- 1997, c. 36, s. 187
- 2001, c. 25, s. 67
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Seizure cancels notice

**[[125]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-125 .sectionLabel}** The seizure under this
Act of anything in respect of which a notice is served under section
124, except as evidence alone, constitutes a cancellation of the notice
where the notice and the seizure are in respect of the same
contravention.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation respecting seizure

**[[126]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-126 .sectionLabel}** Nothing in respect of
which a notice is served under section 124 is, from the time the amount
demanded in the notice is paid or from the time a decision of the
Minister under section 131 is requested in respect of the amount
demanded, subject to seizure under this Act in respect of the same
contravention except as evidence alone.

#### [Review of Seizure, Ascertained Forfeiture or Penalty Assessment]{.HTitleText3} {#h-141785 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No review or appeal

**[[126.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-126.1 .sectionLabel}** Sections 127 to 133
do not apply to a contravention of subsection 40(3) of this Act by a
person referred to in paragraph (c) of that subsection, or to a
contravention of section 32.2 of this Act in circumstances to which
subsection 32.2(6) of this Act applies, or to a contravention of
subsection 95(1), 118(1) or (2), 121(1) or 122(1) of the [Customs
Tariff](/eng/acts/C-54.011).

::: HistoricalNote
- 1995, c. 41, s. 34
- 1997, c. 36, s. 188
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Review of ascertained forfeiture or
penalty assessment

**[[127]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-127 .sectionLabel}** The debt due to Her
Majesty as a result of a notice served under section 109.3 or a demand
under section 124 is final and not subject to review or to be
restrained, prohibited, removed, set aside or otherwise dealt with
except to the extent and in the manner provided by sections 127.1 and
129.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 1 (2nd Supp.), s. 127
- 1993, c. 25, s. 81
- 2001, c. 25, s. 68
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Corrective measures

- **[[127.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-127.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Minister, or any officer
  designated by the President for the purposes of this section, may
  cancel a seizure made under section 110, cancel or reduce a penalty
  assessed under section 109.3 or an amount demanded under section 124
  or refund an amount received under any of sections 117 to 119 within
  90 days after the seizure, assessment or demand, if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the Minister is satisfied that there was no
    contravention; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} there was a contravention but the Minister
    considers that there was an error with respect to the amount
    assessed, collected, demanded or taken as security and that the
    amount should be reduced.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interest

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If an amount is returned to a person under paragraph
  (1)(a), the person shall be given interest on that amount at the
  prescribed rate for the period beginning on the day after the amount
  was originally paid by that person and ending on the day it was
  returned.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 25, s. 68
- 2005, c. 38, s. 81
- 2009, c. 10, s. 13(F)
- 2014, c. 20, s. 172
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-127.1-20090611.html "Link to previous version of section 127.1"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Report to President

**[[128]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-128 .sectionLabel}** Where goods or a
conveyance has been seized under this Act, or a notice has been served
under section 109.3 or 124, the officer who seized the goods or
conveyance or served the notice or caused it to be served shall
forthwith report the circumstances of the case to the President.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 1 (2nd Supp.), s. 128
- 1993, c. 25, s. 81
- 1999, c. 17, s. 127
- 2005, c. 38, s. 85
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-128-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 128"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Request for Minister's decision

- **[[129]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-129 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  following persons may, within 90 days after the date of a seizure or
  the service of a notice, request a decision of the Minister under
  section 131 by giving notice to the Minister in writing or by any
  other means that is satisfactory to the Minister:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any person from whom goods or a conveyance is
    seized under this Act;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any person who owns goods or a conveyance that is
    seized under this Act;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} any person from whom money or security is received
    pursuant to section 117, 118 or 119 in respect of goods or a
    conveyance seized under this Act; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} any person on whom a notice is served under section
    109.3 or 124.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Burden of proof

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The burden of proof that notice was given under
  subsection (1) lies on the person claiming to have given the notice.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 1 (2nd Supp.), s. 129
- 1993, c. 25, s. 82
- 2001, c. 25, s. 69
- 2014, c. 20, s. 173
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-129-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 129"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Extension of time by Minister

- **[[129.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-129.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If no request for a decision of the
  Minister is made under section 129 within the time provided in that
  section, a person may apply in writing to the Minister for an
  extension of the time for making the request and the Minister may
  grant the application.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reasons

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An application must set out the reasons why the
  request was not made on time.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Burden of proof of application

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The burden of proof that an application has been made
  under subsection (1) lies on the person claiming to have made it.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of decision

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Minister must, without delay after making a
  decision in respect of an application, notify the applicant in writing
  of the decision.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditions for granting application

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The application may not be granted unless

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} it is made within one year after the expiration of
    the time provided in section 129; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the applicant demonstrates that

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} within the time provided in section 129, the
      applicant was unable to request a decision or to instruct another
      person to request a decision on the applicant's behalf or the
      applicant had a [bona fide]{.otherLang lang="la"} intention to
      request a decision,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} it would be just and equitable to grant the
      application, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the application was made as soon as
      circumstances permitted.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 25, s. 70
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Extension of time by Federal Court

- **[[129.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-129.2
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A person may apply to the Federal
  Court to have their application under section 129.1 granted if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the Minister dismisses that application; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} ninety days have expired after the application was
    made and the Minister has not notified the person of a decision made
    in respect of it.

  If paragraph (a) applies, the application under this subsection must
  be made within ninety days after the application is dismissed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application process

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The application must be made by filing a copy of the
  application made under section 129.1, and any notice given in respect
  of it, with the Minister and the Administrator of the Court.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers of the Court

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Court may grant or dismiss the application and,
  if it grants the application, may impose any terms that it considers
  just or order that the request under section 129 be deemed to have
  been made on the date the order was made.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditions for granting application

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The application may not be granted unless

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the application under subsection 129.1(1) was made
    within one year after the expiration of the time provided in section
    129; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the person making the application demonstrates that

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} within the time provided in section 129 for
      making a request for a decision of the Minister, the person was
      unable to act or to instruct another person to act in the person's
      name or had a [bona fide]{.otherLang lang="la"} intention to
      request a decision,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} it would be just and equitable to grant the
      application, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the application was made as soon as
      circumstances permitted.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 25, s. 70
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of reasons for action

- **[[130]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-130
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where a decision of the Minister
  under section 131 is requested under section 129, the President shall
  forthwith serve on the person who requested the decision written
  notice of the reasons for the seizure, or for the notice served under
  section 109.3 or 124, in respect of which the decision is requested.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Evidence

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The person on whom a notice is served under
  subsection (1) may, within thirty days after the notice is served,
  furnish such evidence in the matter as he desires to furnish.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Evidence

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Evidence may be given under subsection (2) by
  affidavit made before any person authorized by an Act of Parliament or
  of the legislature of a province to administer oaths or take
  affidavits.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 1 (2nd Supp.), s. 130
- 1993, c. 25, s. 83
- 1999, c. 17, s. 127
- 2001, c. 25, s. 71
- 2005, c. 38, s. 85
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-130-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 130"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Decision of the Minister

- **[[131]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-131
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} After the expiration of the thirty
  days referred to in subsection 130(2), the Minister shall, as soon as
  is reasonably possible having regard to the circumstances, consider
  and weigh the circumstances of the case and decide

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of goods or a conveyance seized or with
    respect to which a notice was served under section 124 on the ground
    that this Act or the regulations were contravened in respect of the
    goods or the conveyance, whether the Act or the regulations were so
    contravened;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a conveyance seized or in respect of
    which a notice was served under section 124 on the ground that it
    was made use of in respect of goods in respect of which this Act or
    the regulations were contravened, whether the conveyance was made
    use of in that way and whether the Act or the regulations were so
    contravened; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a penalty assessed under section
    109.3 against a person for failure to comply with subsection
    109.1(1) or (2) or a provision that is designated under subsection
    109.1(3), whether the person so failed to comply.

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2001, c. 25, s. 72\]]{.Repealed}

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} A person on whom a notice is served under section
  130 may notify the Minister, in writing, that the person will not be
  furnishing evidence under that section and authorize the Minister to
  make a decision without delay in the matter.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of decision

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister shall, forthwith on making a decision
  under subsection (1), serve on the person who requested the decision a
  detailed written notice of the decision.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Judicial review

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Minister's decision under subsection (1) is not
  subject to review or to be restrained, prohibited, removed, set aside
  or otherwise dealt with except to the extent and in the manner
  provided by subsection 135(1).

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 1 (2nd Supp.), s. 131
- 1993, c. 25, s. 84
- 2001, c. 25, s. 72
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Where there is no contravention

- **[[132]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-132
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to this or any other Act of
  Parliament,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} where the Minister decides, under paragraph
    131(1)(a) or (b), that there has been no contravention of this Act
    or the regulations in respect of the goods or conveyance referred to
    in that paragraph, or, under paragraph 131(1)(b), that the
    conveyance referred to in that paragraph was not used in the manner
    described in that paragraph, the Minister shall forthwith authorize
    the removal from custody of the goods or conveyance or the return of
    any money or security taken in respect of the goods or conveyance;
    and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} where, as a result of a decision made by the
    Minister under paragraph 131(1)(c), the Minister decides that a
    penalty that was assessed under section 109.3 is not justified by
    the facts or the law, the Minister shall forthwith cancel the
    assessment of the penalty and authorize the return of any money paid
    on account of the penalty and any interest that was paid under
    section 109.5 in respect of the penalty.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interest on money returned

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where any money is authorized under subsection (1) to
  be returned to any person, there shall be paid to that person, in
  addition to the money returned, interest on the money at the
  prescribed rate for the period beginning on the day after the day the
  money was paid and ending on the day the money is returned.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 1992, c. 28, s. 26\]]{.Repealed}

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 1 (2nd Supp.), s. 132
- 1992, c. 28, s. 26
- 1993, c. 25, s. 85
- 2001, c. 25, s. 73
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Where there is contravention

- **[[133]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-133
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where the Minister decides, under
  paragraph 131(1)(a) or (b), that there has been a contravention of
  this Act or the regulations in respect of the goods or conveyance
  referred to in that paragraph, and, in the case of a conveyance
  referred to in paragraph 131(1)(b), that it was used in the manner
  described in that paragraph, the Minister may, subject to such terms
  and conditions as the Minister may determine,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} return the goods or conveyance on receipt of an
    amount of money of a value equal to an amount determined under
    subsection (2) or (3), as the case may be;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} remit any portion of any money or security taken;
    and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} where the Minister considers that insufficient
    money or security was taken or where no money or security was
    received, demand such amount of money as he considers sufficient,
    not exceeding an amount determined under subsection (4) or (5), as
    the case may be.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers of Minister

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} If the Minister decides under paragraph 131(1)(c)
  that the person failed to comply, the Minister may, subject to any
  terms and conditions that the Minister may determine,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} remit any portion of the penalty assessed under
    section 109.3; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} demand that an additional amount be paid.

  If an additional amount is demanded, the total of the amount assessed
  and the additional amount may not exceed the maximum penalty that
  could be assessed under section 109.3.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Return of goods under paragraph (1)(a)

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Goods may be returned under paragraph (1)(a) on
  receipt of an amount of money of a value equal to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the aggregate of the value for duty of the goods
    and the amount of duties levied thereon, if any, calculated at the
    rates applicable thereto

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} at the time of seizure, if the goods have not
      been accounted for under subsection 32(1), (2) or (5) or if duties
      or additional duties have become due on the goods under paragraph
      32.2(2)(b) in circumstances to which subsection 32.2(6) applies,
      or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} at the time the goods were accounted for under
      subsection 32(1), (2) or (5), in any other case; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} such lesser amount as the Minister may direct.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Return of a conveyance under paragraph
  (1)(a)

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A conveyance may be returned under paragraph (1)(a)
  on receipt of an amount of money of a value equal to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the value of the conveyance at the time of seizure,
    as determined by the Minister; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} such lesser amount as the Minister may direct.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Amount demanded in respect of goods
  under paragraph (1)(c)

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The amount of money that the Minister may demand
  under paragraph (1)(c) in respect of goods shall not exceed an amount
  equal to the aggregate of the value for duty of the goods and the
  amount of duties levied thereon, if any, calculated at the rates
  applicable thereto,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} at the time of seizure or of service of the notice
    under section 124, if the goods have not been accounted for under
    subsection 32(1), (2) or (5) or if duties or additional duties have
    become due on the goods under paragraph 32.2(2)(b) in circumstances
    to which subsection 32.2(6) applies; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} at the time the goods were accounted for under
    subsection 32(1), (2) or (5), in any other case.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Amount demanded in respect of
  conveyance under paragraph (1)(c)

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The amount of money that the Minister may demand
  under paragraph (1)(c) in respect of a conveyance shall not exceed an
  amount equal to the value of the conveyance at the time of seizure or
  of service of the notice under section 124, as determined by the
  Minister.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Value substituted for value for duty

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of calculating the amount of money
  referred to in subsection (2) or (4), where the value for duty of
  goods cannot be ascertained, the value of the goods at the time of
  seizure or of service of the notice under section 124, as determined
  by the Minister, may be substituted for the value for duty thereof.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interest

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} If an amount of money is demanded under paragraph
  (1)(c) or (1.1)(b), the person to whom the demand is made shall pay
  the amount demanded together with interest at the prescribed rate for
  the period beginning on the day after the notice is served under
  subsection 131(2) and ending on the day the amount has been paid in
  full, calculated on the outstanding balance of the amount. However,
  interest is not payable if the amount demanded is paid in full within
  thirty days after the notice is served.

- [(8)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 1992, c. 28, s. 27\]]{.Repealed}

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 1 (2nd Supp.), s. 133
- 1992, c. 28, s. 27
- 1993, c. 25, s. 86
- 1995, c. 41, s. 35
- 1997, c. 36, s. 189
- 2001, c. 25, s. 74
:::

**[[134]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-134 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed, 1998, c.
19, s. 263\]]{.Repealed}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Federal Court

- **[[135]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-135 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person who requests a decision of the Minister under section 131 may,
  within ninety days after being notified of the decision, appeal the
  decision by way of an action in the Federal Court in which that person
  is the plaintiff and the Minister is the defendant.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Ordinary action

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The [Federal Courts Act](/eng/acts/F-7) and the rules
  made under that Act applicable to ordinary actions apply in respect of
  actions instituted under subsection (1) except as varied by special
  rules made in respect of such actions.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 1 (2nd Supp.), s. 135
- 1990, c. 8, s. 49
- 2002, c. 8, s. 134
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-135-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 135"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Restoration of goods pending appeal

**[[136]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-136 .sectionLabel}** Where an appeal is
taken by the Crown from any judgment that orders the Crown to give or
return anything that has been seized under this Act to any person, the
execution of the judgment shall not be suspended if the person to whom
the goods are ordered given or returned gives such security to the Crown
as the court that rendered the judgment, or a judge thereof, considers
sufficient to ensure delivery of the goods or the full value thereof to
the Crown if the judgment so appealed is reversed.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Service of notices

**[[137]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-137 .sectionLabel}** The service of the
President's notice under section 130 or the notice of the Minister's
decision under section 131 is sufficient if it is sent by registered
mail addressed to the person on whom it is to be served at his latest
known address.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 1 (2nd Supp.), s. 137
- 1999, c. 17, s. 127
- 2005, c. 38, s. 85
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-137-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 137"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Third Party Claims]{.HTitleText3} {#h-141937 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Third party claims

- **[[138]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-138 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If
  goods or a conveyance is seized as forfeit under this Act or if a
  conveyance is detained under subsection 97.25(2), any person, other
  than the person in whose possession it was when seized or detained,
  who claims an interest in it as owner, mortgagee, hypothecary
  creditor, lien-holder or holder of any like interest may, within
  ninety days after the seizure or detention, apply for a decision by
  the Minister under section 139.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application procedure

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A person may apply for a decision by giving notice to
  the Minister in writing or by any other means that is satisfactory to
  the Minister.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Burden of proof of application

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The burden of proof that an application has been made
  under subsection (1) lies on the person claiming to have made it.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Provision of evidence

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A person who applies under subsection (1) must
  provide evidence that relates to their interest in the seized or
  detained goods or conveyance and any other evidence requested by the
  Minister in respect of that interest.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Manner of giving evidence

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Evidence may be given under subsection (4) by
  affidavit made before any person authorized by an Act of Parliament or
  of the legislature of a province to administer oaths or take
  affidavits.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Late applications

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may accept an application made within
  one year after the expiration of the ninety days referred to in
  subsection (1) by a person who has not claimed an interest in the
  seized or detained goods or conveyance within those ninety days.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditions for late applications

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} When making an application under subsection (6), the
  person must demonstrate to the Minister that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} within the time provided in subsection (1) for
    making an application the person

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} was unable to act or to instruct another person
      to act in the person's name, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} had a [bona fide]{.otherLang lang="la"}
      intention to apply under that subsection;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} it would be just and equitable to grant the
    application; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the application was made as soon as circumstances
    permitted.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 1 (2nd Supp.), s. 138
- 1992, c. 1, s. 62, c. 51, s. 45
- 1998, c. 30, s. 14
- 1999, c. 3, s. 60, c. 17, s. 127
- 2001, c. 25, s. 75
- 2014, c. 20, s. 174
- 2015, c. 3, s. 62(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-138-20140619.html "Link to previous version of section 138"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Decision of Minister

**[[139]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-139 .sectionLabel}** The Minister must
decide an application made under section 138 without delay and, if the
Minister is satisfied that the following conditions are met, must make a
determination that the applicant's interest in the goods or conveyance
is not affected by the seizure or detention and as to the nature and
extent of the applicant's interest at the time of the contravention or
use:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the applicant acquired the interest in good faith
  before the contravention or use;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the applicant is innocent of any complicity or
  collusion in the contravention or use; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the applicant exercised all reasonable care in
  respect of any person permitted to obtain possession of the goods or
  conveyance in order to satisfy the applicant that it was not likely to
  be used in a contravention or, if the applicant is a mortgagee,
  hypothecary creditor or lien-holder, the applicant exercised that care
  in relation to the mortgagor, hypothecary debtor or lien-giver.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 1 (2nd Supp.), s. 139
- 2001, c. 25, s. 75
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order

- **[[139.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-139.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A person who makes an application
  under section 138 may, within ninety days after being notified of the
  decision, apply for an order under this section by giving notice in
  writing to the court.

- Meaning of [[court]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In this section, [[court]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the Province of Ontario, the Superior Court of
    Justice;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the Province of Quebec, the Superior Court;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in the Provinces of Nova Scotia, British Columbia
    and Prince Edward Island, Yukon and the Northwest Territories, the
    Supreme Court;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} in the Provinces of New Brunswick, Manitoba,
    Saskatchewan and Alberta, the Court of Queen's Bench;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} in the Province of Newfoundland and Labrador, the
    Trial Division of the Supreme Court; and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} in Nunavut, the Nunavut Court of Justice.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Date of hearing

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A judge of the court must fix a day, not less than
  thirty days after the application has been made, for the hearing of
  the application.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice to Minister

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The applicant, no later than fifteen days before the
  day fixed for the hearing, must serve notice of the application and of
  the hearing on the Minister, or an officer designated by the Minister
  for the purposes of this section.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Service by registered mail

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Service of the notice is sufficient if it is sent by
  registered mail addressed to the Minister.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The applicant is entitled to an order declaring that
  the applicant's interest is not affected by the seizure or detention
  and declaring the nature and extent of the applicant's interest at the
  time of the contravention or use if, on the hearing of the
  application, the court is satisfied that the applicant

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} acquired the interest in good faith prior to the
    contravention or use;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is innocent of any complicity or collusion in the
    contravention or use; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} exercised all reasonable care in respect of any
    person permitted to obtain possession of the goods or conveyance in
    order to satisfy the applicant that it was not likely to be used in
    a contravention or, if the applicant is a mortgagee, hypothecary
    creditor or lien-holder, that the applicant exercised that care in
    relation to the mortgagor, hypothecary debtor or lien-giver.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 25, s. 75
- 2002, c. 7, s. 272
- 2009, c. 10, s. 14(F)
- 2015, c. 3, s. 63
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-139.1-20090611.html "Link to previous version of section 139.1"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appeal

- **[[140]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-140 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person who makes an application under section 139.1 or the Crown may
  appeal to the court of appeal from an order made under that section
  and the appeal shall be asserted, heard and decided according to the
  ordinary procedure governing appeals to the court of appeal from
  orders or judgments of a court.

- Definition of [[court of appeal]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In this section, [[court of
  appeal]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means, in the province in which an order
  under this section is made, the court of appeal for that province as
  defined in section 2 of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46).

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 1 (2nd Supp.), s. 140
- 2001, c. 25, s. 76
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Goods or conveyance given to applicant

- **[[141]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-141 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  President, on application by a person whose interest in a conveyance
  detained under subsection 97.25(2) or in goods or a conveyance seized
  as forfeit under this Act has been determined under section 139 or
  ordered under section 139.1 or 140 to be unaffected by the seizure or
  detention, shall direct that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of goods or a conveyance the forfeiture
    of which has become final, the goods or conveyance, as the case may
    be, be given to the applicant; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a conveyance detained under
    subsection 97.25(2), the conveyance be given to the applicant.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Amount paid if goods or conveyance sold

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} If goods or a conveyance that is to be given to the
  applicant has been sold or disposed of, an amount calculated on the
  basis of the interest of the applicant in the goods or conveyance at
  the time of the contravention or use, as determined under section 139
  or ordered under section 139.1 or 140, shall be paid to the applicant.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limit on amount paid

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The total amount paid under subsection (1.1) in
  respect of goods or a conveyance shall, if the goods or conveyance was
  sold or otherwise disposed of under this Act, not exceed the proceeds
  of the sale or disposition, if any, less any costs incurred by Her
  Majesty in respect of the goods or conveyance, and, if there are no
  proceeds of disposition, no payment shall be made pursuant to
  subsection (1.1).

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 1 (2nd Supp.), s. 141
- 1999, c. 17, s. 127
- 2001, c. 25, s. 77
- 2005, c. 38, s. 85
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-141-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 141"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Disposal of Things Abandoned or Forfeit]{.HTitleText2} {#h-142009 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disposal of things abandoned or forfeit

- **[[142]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-142
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Unless the thing is spirits,
  specially denatured alcohol, a restricted formulation, wine, raw leaf
  tobacco, an excise stamp, a tobacco product or a vaping product,
  anything that has been abandoned to Her Majesty in right of Canada
  under this Act and anything the forfeiture of which is final under
  this Act shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} where the Minister deems it appropriate, be
    exported;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} where the importation thereof is prohibited, or
    where the Minister considers the thing to be unsuitable for sale or
    of insufficient value to justify a sale, be disposed of in such
    manner, otherwise than by sale, as the Minister may direct; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in any other case, be sold by public auction or
    public tender or by the Minister of Public Works and Government
    Services pursuant to the [Surplus Crown Assets Act](/eng/acts/S-27),
    subject to such regulations as may be prescribed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duties removed

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Any goods that are disposed of pursuant to
  subsection (1) are, from the time of disposal, no longer charged with
  duties.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 1 (2nd Supp.), s. 142
- 1996, c. 16, s. 60
- 2002, c. 22, s. 340
- 2007, c. 18, s. 139
- 2010, c. 12, s. 52
- [2022, c. 10, s. 91](#1356494-1376935){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1356494-1376935.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-142-20100712.html "Link to previous version of section 142"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Dealing with abandoned or forfeited
alcohol, etc.

- **[[142.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-142.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If spirits, specially denatured
  alcohol, a restricted formulation, wine, raw leaf tobacco, a tobacco
  product or a vaping product is abandoned or finally forfeited under
  this Act, the Minister may sell, destroy or otherwise deal with it.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Dealing with abandoned or forfeited
  excise stamps

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} If an excise stamp is abandoned or finally
  forfeited under this Act, the Minister may destroy or otherwise deal
  with it.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Restriction

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to the regulations, the sale under
  subsection (1) of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} spirits or specially denatured alcohol may only be
    to a spirits licensee;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} wine may only be to a wine licensee;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} raw leaf tobacco or a tobacco product may only be
    to a tobacco licensee;

  - [(c.1)]{.lawlabel} a vaping product may only be to a vaping product
    licensee; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a restricted formulation may only be to a licensed
    user.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2002, c. 22, s. 341
- 2007, c. 18, s. 140
- 2010, c. 12, s. 53
- [2022, c. 10, s. 92](#1356496-1376939){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1356496-1376939.html"}
:::
