Version](section-142.1-20100712.html "Link to previous version of section 142.1"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[143 to 147]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-143_to_147
.sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed, 2001, c. 25, s. 78\]]{.Repealed}

### [Collection of Duties on Mail]{.HTitleText2} {#h-142034 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition

- **[[147.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-147.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In this section,
  [[Corporation]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the Canada Post Corporation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsections (3) to (13) apply to mail except as may
  be provided in regulations made under paragraph (14)(e).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Collection agreement

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Minister and the Corporation may enter into an
  agreement in writing whereby the Minister authorizes the Corporation
  to collect, as agent of the Minister, duties in respect of mail and
  the Corporation agrees to collect the duties as agent of the Minister.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Terms and conditions

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} An agreement made under subsection (3) relating to
  the collection of duties in respect of mail may provide for the terms
  and conditions under which and the period during which the Corporation
  is authorized to collect the duties and for other matters in relation
  to the administration of this Act in respect of such mail.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Authorization by Corporation

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The Corporation may authorize in writing any person
  to collect, as its agent, duties under terms and conditions consistent
  with those provided for in the agreement made under subsection (3) and
  during a period not exceeding the period provided for in that
  agreement.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Liability to pay duties

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Where the Corporation has entered into an agreement
  under subsection (3), the Corporation shall pay to the Receiver
  General, within the prescribed time and in the prescribed manner, as
  an amount due to Her Majesty in right of Canada in respect of mail to
  which the agreement applies, the greater of the duties collected by
  the Corporation in respect of the mail and the duties required to be
  collected in respect of the mail by the Corporation under the
  agreement, unless

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the Corporation establishes to the satisfaction of
    the Minister that the mail has not been delivered and that the mail

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} is no longer in Canada, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} was destroyed;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} duties have not been collected by the Corporation
    in respect of the mail, the mail has not been delivered and a
    request for a re-determination or further re-determination has been
    made under subsection 60(1) in respect of the mail; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in any other case, duties have not been collected
    by the Corporation in respect of the mail, the mail has not been
    delivered and the period in which a request for a re-determination
    or further re-determination may be made under subsection 60(1) in
    respect of the mail has not expired.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Not public money

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} An amount required to be paid to the Receiver General
  under subsection (6) shall be deemed not to be public money for the
  purposes of the [Financial Administration Act](/eng/acts/F-11) until
  the amount has been so paid.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interest

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} Where an amount that the Corporation is required to
  pay under subsection (6) has not been paid within the time within
  which it is required to be paid under that subsection, the Corporation
  shall pay to the Receiver General, in addition to that amount,
  interest at the specified rate for the period beginning on the first
  day after that time and ending on the day the amount has been paid in
  full, calculated on the outstanding balance of the amount.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Detention of mail

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} Any person who is authorized to collect duties in
  respect of mail may detain the mail until the duties thereon have been
  paid to the Corporation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Fees

  [(10)]{.lawlabel} Subject to any regulations made under subsection
  (14), mail is charged with prescribed fees from the time of its
  importation until such time as the fees are paid or as the fees are
  otherwise removed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Payment of fees

  [(11)]{.lawlabel} The importer or owner of mail that is charged with
  fees under subsection (10) shall pay the fees at the time of the
  payment of the duties on the mail.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Collection of fees

  [(12)]{.lawlabel} Where the Corporation or an agent of the Corporation
  is authorized to collect duties in respect of mail, the Corporation or
  the agent may collect the fees with which the mail is charged under
  subsection (10) and may detain the mail until the fees have been paid.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Fees belong to Corporation

  [(13)]{.lawlabel} Fees collected under subsection (12) are property of
  the Corporation and shall be deemed not to be public money for the
  purposes of the [Financial Administration Act](/eng/acts/F-11).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

  [(14)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may make regulations

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} prescribing times for the purposes of subsection
    (6);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} prescribing the manner of payment for the purposes
    of subsection (6);

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} prescribing fees for the purposes of subsection
    (10);

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} prescribing mail that is not charged with fees
    under subsection (10) or prescribing circumstances in which mail is
    not charged with fees under that subsection; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} prescribing mail to which any of subsections (3)
    to (13) does not apply or prescribing circumstances in which any of
    those subsections does not apply to mail.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 28, s. 29
- 1997, c. 36, s. 190
:::

### [Evidence]{.HTitleText2} {#h-142076 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Proof of service by registered mail

- **[[148]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-148
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where a notice required by this Act
  or a regulation is sent by registered mail, an affidavit of an officer
  sworn before a commissioner or other person authorized to take
  affidavits setting out

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} that the officer has charge of the appropriate
    records,

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} that he has knowledge of the facts in the
    particular case,

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} that such a notice was sent by registered letter on
    a named day to the person to whom it was addressed (indicating such
    address), and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} that he identifies as exhibits attached to the
    affidavit the post office certificate of registration of the letter
    or a true copy of the relevant portion thereof and a true copy of
    the notice

  shall be received, in the absence of evidence to the contrary, as
  proof of the sending and of the notice.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Proof of personal service

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where a notice required by this Act or a regulation
  is given by personal service, an affidavit of an officer sworn before
  a commissioner or other person authorized to take affidavits setting
  out

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} that the officer has charge of the appropriate
    records,

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} that he has knowledge of the facts in the
    particular case,

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} that such a notice was served personally on a named
    day on the person to whom it was directed, and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} that he identifies as an exhibit attached to the
    affidavit a true copy of the notice

  shall be received, in the absence of evidence to the contrary, as
  proof of the personal service and of the notice.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Members of partnerships

- **[[148.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-148.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of this Act,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a reference in any notice or other document to the
    firm name of a partnership is to be read as a reference to all the
    members of the partnership; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any notice or other document is deemed to have been
    provided to each member of a partnership if the notice or other
    document is mailed to, served on or otherwise sent to the
    partnership

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} at its latest known address or place of business,
      or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} at the latest known address

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} if it is a limited partnership, of any member
        of the limited partnership whose liability as a member is not
        limited, or

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} in any other case, of any member of the
        partnership.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Members of unincorporated bodies

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of this Act,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a reference in any notice or other document to the
    firm name of an unincorporated body is to be read as a reference to
    all the members of the body; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any notice or other document is deemed to have been
    provided to each member of an unincorporated body if the notice or
    other document is mailed to, served on or otherwise sent to the body
    at its latest known address or place of business.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 25, s. 79
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Date of notice by mail

**[[149]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-149 .sectionLabel}** For the purposes of
this Act, the date on which a notice is given pursuant to this Act or
the regulations shall, where it is given by mail, be deemed to be the
date of mailing of the notice, and the date of mailing shall, in the
absence of any evidence to the contrary, be deemed to be the day
appearing from such notice to be the date thereof unless called into
question by the Minister or by some person acting for him or Her
Majesty.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Proof of no appeal

**[[149.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-149.1 .sectionLabel}** An affidavit of an
officer, sworn before a commissioner or other person authorized to take
affidavits, setting out that the officer has charge of the appropriate
records and has knowledge of the practice of the Agency or the Canada
Revenue Agency, as the case may be, and that an examination of the
records shows that a notice of assessment under Part V.1 was mailed or
otherwise sent to a person under this Act and that, after careful
examination and search of the records, the officer has been unable to
find that a notice of objection or of appeal from the assessment was
received within the time allowed for the notice, is evidence of the
statements contained in the affidavit.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 25, s. 80
- 2005, c. 38, s. 82
- 2009, c. 10, s. 15(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-149.1-20051212.html "Link to previous version of section 149.1"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Objection or appeal

**[[149.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-149.2 .sectionLabel}** If a person who is
required under this Act to keep records serves a notice of objection or
is a party to an appeal or reference under Part V.1, the person shall
retain, until the objection, appeal or reference and any appeal from it
is finally disposed of, every record that pertains to the subject-matter
of the objection, appeal or reference.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 25, s. 80
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Copies of documents

**[[150]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-150 .sectionLabel}** Copies of documents,
including electronic documents, made under this or any other Act of
Parliament prohibiting, controlling or regulating the importation or
exportation of goods, that are duly certified by an officer are
admissible in evidence in any proceeding taken under this Act in the
same manner as if they were the originals of such documents.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 1 (2nd Supp.), s. 150
- [2022, c. 10, s. 328](#1357575-1447472){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1357575-1447472.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-150-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 150"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}False information in documents

**[[151]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-151 .sectionLabel}** In any proceeding taken
pursuant to this Act, the production or the proof of the existence of
more than one document made or sent by or on behalf of the same person
in which the same goods are mentioned as bearing different prices or
given different names or descriptions is, in the absence of evidence to
the contrary, proof that any such document was intended to be used to
evade compliance with this Act or the payment of duties under this Act.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Burden of proof of importation or
exportation on Her Majesty

- **[[152]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-152 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In
  any proceeding under this Act relating to the importation or
  exportation of goods, the burden of proof of the importation or
  exportation of the goods lies on Her Majesty.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Proof of importation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of subsection (1), proof of the
  foreign origin of goods is, in the absence of evidence to the
  contrary, proof of the importation of the goods.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Burden of proof on other party

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (4), in any proceeding under
  this Act, the burden of proof in any question relating to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the identity or origin of any goods,

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the manner, time or place of importation or
    exportation of any goods,

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the payment of duties on any goods, or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the compliance with any of the provisions of this
    Act or the regulations in respect of any goods

  lies on the person, other than Her Majesty, who is a party to the
  proceeding or the person who is accused of an offence, and not on Her
  Majesty.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception in case of prosecution

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} In any prosecution under this Act, the burden of
  proof in any question relating to the matters referred to in
  paragraphs (3)(a) to (d) lies on the person who is accused of an
  offence, and not on Her Majesty, only if the Crown has established
  that the facts or circumstances concerned are within the knowledge of
  the accused or are or were within his means to know.

### [Prohibitions, Offences and Punishment]{.HTitleText2} {#h-142135 .Subheading}

#### [General]{.HTitleText3} {#h-142136 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}False statements, evasion of duties

**[[153]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-153 .sectionLabel}** No person shall

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} make, or participate in, assent to or acquiesce in
  the making of, false or deceptive statements in a statement or answer
  made orally or in writing pursuant to this Act or the regulations;

- [(a.1)]{.lawlabel} make, or participate in, assent to or acquiesce in
  the making of, false or deceptive statements in an application for an
  advance ruling under section 43.1 or a certificate referred to in
  section 97.1;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} to avoid compliance with this Act or the regulations,

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} destroy, alter, mutilate, secrete or dispose of
    records or books of account,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} make, or participate in, assent to or acquiesce in
    the making of, false or deceptive entries in records or books of
    account, or

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} omit, or participate in, assent to or acquiesce
    in the omission of, a material particular from records or books of
    account; or

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} wilfully, in any manner, evade or attempt to evade
  compliance with any provision of this Act or evade or attempt to evade
  the payment of duties under this Act.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 1 (2nd Supp.), s. 153
- 1988, c. 65, s. 80
- 1993, c. 44, s. 105
- 1996, c. 33, s. 39
- 1997, c. 14, s. 46
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Hindering an officer

**[[153.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-153.1 .sectionLabel}** No person shall,
physically or otherwise, do or attempt to do any of the following:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} interfere with or molest an officer doing anything
  that the officer is authorized to do under this Act; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} hinder or prevent an officer from doing anything that
  the officer is authorized to do under this Act.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 17, s. 255
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Misdescription of goods in accounting
documents

**[[154]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-154 .sectionLabel}** No person shall include
in any document used for the purpose of accounting under section 32 a
description of goods that does not correspond with the goods so
described.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Keeping, acquiring, disposing of goods
illegally imported

**[[155]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-155 .sectionLabel}** No person shall,
without lawful authority or excuse, the proof of which lies on him, have
in his possession, purchase, sell, exchange or otherwise acquire or
dispose of any imported goods in respect of which the provisions of this
or any other Act of Parliament that prohibits, controls or regulates the
importation of goods have been contravened.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Possession of blank documents

**[[156]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-156 .sectionLabel}** No person shall,
without lawful authority or excuse, the proof of which lies on him, send
or bring into Canada or have in his possession any form, document or
other writing that is wholly or partly blank and is capable of being
completed and used in accounting for imported goods pursuant to this
Act, where the form, document or other writing bears any certificate,
signature or other mark that is intended to show that such form,
document or writing is correct or authentic.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Opening and unpacking goods; breaking
seals

**[[157]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-157 .sectionLabel}** No person shall,
without lawful authority or excuse, the proof of which lies on him,

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} open or unpack, or cause to be opened or unpacked,
  any package of imported goods that has not been released; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} break or tamper with, or cause to be broken or
  tampered with, any seals, locks or fastenings that have been placed on
  goods, conveyances, bonded warehouses or duty free shops pursuant to
  this Act or the regulations.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Officers, etc., of corporations

**[[158]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-158 .sectionLabel}** Where a corporation
commits an offence under this Act, any officer, director or agent of the
corporation who directed, authorized, assented to, acquiesced in or
participated in the commission of the offence is a party to and guilty
of the offence and is liable on conviction to the punishment provided
for the offence whether or not the corporation has been prosecuted or
convicted.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Smuggling --- into Canada

- **[[159]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-159
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every person commits an offence who
  smuggles or attempts to smuggle into Canada, whether clandestinely or
  not, any goods that are subject to duties, or any goods the
  importation of which is prohibited, controlled or regulated under this
  or any other Act of Parliament.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Smuggling --- out of Canada

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every person commits an offence who smuggles or
  attempts to smuggle out of Canada, whether clandestinely or not, any
  goods that are subject to duties, or any goods the exportation of
  which is prohibited, controlled or regulated under this or any other
  Act of Parliament.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 1 (2nd Supp.), s. 159
- [2018, c. 30, s. 7](#1041182-1186157){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1041182-1186157.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-159-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 159"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offences re marking of goods

**[[159.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-159.1 .sectionLabel}** No person shall

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} fail to mark imported goods in the manner referred to
  in section 35.01;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} mark imported goods in a deceptive manner so as to
  mislead another person as to the country of origin or geographic
  origin of the goods; or

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} with intent to conceal the information given by or
  contained in the mark, alter, deface, remove or destroy a mark on
  imported goods made as required by the regulations made under
  subsection 19(2) of the [Customs Tariff](/eng/acts/C-54.011).

::: HistoricalNote
- 1993, c. 44, s. 106
- 1997, c. 36, s. 191
- 2001, c. 25, s. 81
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}General offence and punishment

- **[[160]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-160
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every person who contravenes section
  11, 12, 13, 15 or 16, subsection 20(1), section 31 or 40, subsection
  43(2), section 94, subsection 95(1) or (3), 103(3) or 107(2) or
  section 153, 155, 156 or 159.1 or commits an offence under subsection
  159(1) or (2) or knowingly contravenes an order referred to in
  subsection 107(11)

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is guilty of an offence punishable on summary
    conviction and liable to a fine of not more than fifty thousand
    dollars or to imprisonment for a term not exceeding six months or to
    both that fine and that imprisonment; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is guilty of an indictable offence and liable to a
    fine of not more than five hundred thousand dollars or to
    imprisonment for a term not exceeding five years or to both that
    fine and that imprisonment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Court order --- subsection 43(2)

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a person has been convicted by a court of an
  offence under subsection (1) for a contravention of subsection 43(2),
  the court may make any order that it considers appropriate in order to
  enforce compliance with that subsection.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 1 (2nd Supp.), s. 160
- 1993, c. 25, s. 88, c. 44, s. 107
- 2001, c. 25, s. 82
- [2018, c. 30, s. 8](#1041184-1186164){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1041184-1186164.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-160-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 160"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Penalty for hindering an officer

**[[160.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-160.1 .sectionLabel}** Every person who
contravenes section 153.1 is guilty of an offence and, in addition to
any penalty otherwise provided, is liable on summary conviction to

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} a fine of not less than \$1,000 and not more than
  \$25,000; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} both a fine described in paragraph (a) and
  imprisonment for a term not exceeding twelve months.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 17, s. 256
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Summary conviction offence and punishment

**[[161]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-161 .sectionLabel}** Every person who
contravenes any of the provisions of this Act not otherwise provided for
in section 160 is guilty of an offence punishable on summary conviction
and liable to a fine of not more than twenty-five thousand dollars and
not less than one thousand dollars or to imprisonment for a term not
exceeding six months or to both fine and imprisonment.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 1 (2nd Supp.), s. 161
- 2001, c. 25, s. 83
:::

#### [Procedure]{.HTitleText3} {#h-142194 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Venue

**[[162]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-162 .sectionLabel}** A prosecution for an
offence under this Act may be instituted, heard, tried or determined in
the place in which the offence was committed or in which the
subject-matter of the prosecution arose or in any place in which the
accused is apprehended or happens to be.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation period in summary convictions

**[[163]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-163 .sectionLabel}** Proceedings may be
instituted by way of summary conviction in respect of offences under
this Act at any time within but not later than three years after the
time when the subject-matter of the proceedings arose.

**[[163.1 to 163.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-163.1_to_163.3
.sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed, 2001, c. 32, s. 62\]]{.Repealed}

## [PART VI.1]{.HLabel1}[Enforcement of Criminal Offences Other than Offences Under This Act]{.HTitleText1} {#h-142200 .Part}

### [Powers of Designated Officers]{.HTitleText2} {#h-142201 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Designation by President

- **[[163.4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-163.4
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The President may designate any
  officer for the purposes of this Part and shall provide the officer
  with a certificate of designation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Admissibility of certificate

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A certificate of designation is admissible in
  evidence as proof of an officer's designation without proof of the
  signature or official character of the person appearing to have signed
  it.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 7, s. 1
- 2005, c. 38, s. 83
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-163.4-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 163.4"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers of designated officers

- **[[163.5]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-163.5
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In addition to the powers conferred
  on an officer for the enforcement of this Act, a designated officer
  who is at a customs office and is performing the normal duties of an
  officer or is acting in accordance with section 99.1 has, in relation
  to a criminal offence under any other Act of Parliament, the powers
  and obligations of a peace officer under sections 495 and 497 and
  paragraphs 498(1)(a) and (b) of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46),
  and subsections 495(3) and 498(3) of that Act apply to the designated
  officer as if they were a peace officer.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers relating to impaired driving
  offences

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A designated officer who is at a customs office
  performing the normal duties of an officer or is acting in accordance
  with section 99.1 has the powers and obligations of a peace officer
  under sections 320.27 to 320.29 of the [Criminal
  Code](/eng/acts/C-46). If, by demand, they require a person to provide
  samples of blood or breath or to submit to an evaluation, they may
  also require the person to accompany a peace officer referred to in
  paragraph (c) of the definition [[peace
  officer]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} in section 2 of that Act, for that
  purpose.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Power to detain

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A designated officer who arrests a person in the
  exercise of the powers conferred under subsection (1) may detain the
  person until the person can be placed in the custody of a peace
  officer referred to in paragraph (c) of the definition [[peace
  officer]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} in section 2 of the [Criminal
  Code](/eng/acts/C-46).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation on powers

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A designated officer may not use any power conferred
  on the officer for the enforcement of this Act for the sole purpose of
  looking for evidence of a criminal offence under any other Act of
  Parliament.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 7, s. 1
- 2001, c. 25, s. 84
- 2008, c. 6, s. 59
- 2018, c. 21, ss. 11, 45
- [2019, c. 25, s. 392](#1148136-1214495){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1148136-1214495.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-163.5-20181218.html "Link to previous version of section 163.5"){.PITLink}
:::

## [PART VII]{.HLabel1}[Regulations]{.HTitleText1} {#h-142220 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

- **[[164]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-164 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Governor in Council may make regulations

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 1998, c. 19, s. 264\]]{.Repealed}

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2009, c. 10, s. 16\]]{.Repealed}

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} requiring the payment of costs incurred for the
    inspection of records held in a place outside of Canada and
    respecting the manner of determining those costs and the time and
    manner in which the costs must be paid;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} authorizing the collection of information or
    evidence in order to facilitate the determination of whether any
    duties are owing or may become owing on imported goods and the
    amount of such duties;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} prescribing the conditions under which
    non-residents may import goods, including the bonds or other
    security that may be required, defining the term
    [[non-residents]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} for the purpose of this
    paragraph and exempting any person or class of persons or any goods
    or class of goods from the application of such conditions;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} prescribing the methods to be followed in
    determining the tariff classification of sugar, molasses and sugar
    syrup, and specifying the instruments, standards and appliances to
    be used in such determinations;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} prescribing the manner of ascertaining the
    alcoholic content of wines, spirits or alcoholic liquors for the
    purpose of determining the tariff classification thereof;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} prescribing how the coasting trade shall be
    regulated in any case or class of cases and exempting any case or
    class of cases, subject to any condition that the Governor in
    Council sees fit to impose, from any of the requirements of this Act
    that the Governor in Council deems it inexpedient to enforce with
    respect to vessels engaged in such trade;

  - [(h.1)]{.lawlabel} defining any term or expression that is by any
    provision of this Act to have a meaning assigned by regulation;

  - [(h.2)]{.lawlabel} respecting the sale of alcohol, a tobacco
    product, raw leaf tobacco, specially denatured alcohol, a restricted
    formulation or a vaping product detained, seized, abandoned or
    forfeited under this Act;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} prescribing anything that is by any provision of
    this Act to be prescribed by the Governor in Council; and

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} generally, to carry out the purposes and provisions
    of this Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may, on the recommendation
  of the Minister, make regulations for the purpose of the uniform
  interpretation, application and administration of a protocol, chapter
  or provision --- set out in column 2 of Part 5 of the schedule --- in
  an agreement set out in column 1, and any other matters that may be
  agreed on from time to time by the parties to that agreement.

- [(1.2) to (1.5)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2012, c. 18, s.
  30\]]{.Repealed}

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations prescribing rate of
  interest

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may, on the recommendation of
  the Minister of Finance, make regulations prescribing a rate of
  interest or rules for determining a rate of interest for the purposes
  of any provision of this Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations under paragraph (1)(i) ---
  section 3.5

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The regulations made under paragraph (1)(i) for the
  purposes of section 3.5 may distinguish among sums according to their
  amount and the class of goods to which those sums relate.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2001, c. 25, s. 85\]]{.Repealed}

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 1 (2nd Supp.), s. 164
- 1988, c. 65, s. 81
- 1992, c. 28, s. 30, c. 31, s. 22
- 1993, c. 44, s. 108
- 1994, c. 47, s. 72
- 1995, c. 41, s. 36
- 1996, c. 33, s. 40
- 1997, c. 14, s. 47
- 1998, c. 19, s. 264
- 2001, c. 25, s. 85, c. 28, s. 30
- 2007, c. 18, s. 141
- 2009, c. 6, s. 29, c. 10, s. 16, c. 16, ss. 35, 56
- 2010, c. 4, s. 29
- 2012, c. 18, s. 30
- 2017, c. 6, s. 85
- [2022, c. 10, s. 93](#1356501-1376941){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1356501-1376941.html"}
- [2022, c. 10, s. 329](#1357577-1492818){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1357577-1492818.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-164-20221001.html "Link to previous version of section 164"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Incorporation by reference

**[[164.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-164.1 .sectionLabel}** A regulation made
under this Act may incorporate by reference any material regardless of
its source and either as it exists on a particular date or as amended
from time to time.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2009, c. 10, s. 17
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibition or regulation of importation

**[[165]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-165 .sectionLabel}** Where at any time it
appears to the satisfaction of the Governor in Council on a report from
the Minister that goods, the exportation of which from any country is
the subject of an arrangement or commitment between the Government of
Canada and the government of that country, are being imported in a
manner that circumvents the arrangement or commitment, the Governor in
Council may, by regulation, prohibit or otherwise control the
importation of goods to which the arrangement or commitment relates.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Bonds and security

- **[[166]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-166 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Governor in Council may make regulations

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} prescribing the amount or authorizing the Minister
    to determine the amount of any deposit, bond or other security
    required to be given under this Act or the regulations; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} prescribing the nature and the terms and conditions
    of any such deposit, bond or other security.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Forms

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Any deposit, bond or other security required under
  this Act shall be in a form satisfactory to the Minister.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 1 (2nd Supp.), s. 166
- [2022, c. 10, s. 330](#1357579-1492825){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1357579-1492825.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-166-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 166"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Special services

- **[[167]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-167 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Governor in Council may make regulations prescribing

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} what services performed by officers at the request
    of a person in charge of imported goods or goods destined for
    exportation shall be considered to be special services;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the charges, if any, that are payable for special
    services by the person requesting them; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the terms and conditions on which special services
    shall be performed, including the taking of such bonds or other
    security as may be prescribed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deeming provision

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Anything that is required under this Act or the
  regulations to be done at a customs office, sufferance warehouse,
  bonded warehouse or duty free shop that is done at another place as a
  result of a special service shall be deemed, for the purposes of this
  Act or the regulations, to have been done at a customs office,
  sufferance warehouse, bonded warehouse or duty free shop, as the case
  may be.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Retroactive effect

**[[167.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-167.1 .sectionLabel}** Where a regulation
made under a provision of this Act provides that the regulation is to
come into force on a day earlier than the day it is registered under
section 6 of the [Statutory Instruments Act](/eng/acts/S-22), the
regulation shall come into force on that earlier day if the regulation

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} has a relieving effect only;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} gives effect, in whole or in part, to a public
  announcement made on or before that earlier day;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} corrects an ambiguous or deficient enactment that was
  not made in accordance with the objects of this Act or the regulations
  made by the Governor in Council under this Act; or

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} is consequential on an amendment to this Act that is
  applicable before the day the regulation is registered under section 6
  of the [Statutory Instruments Act](/eng/acts/S-22).

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 28, s. 31
:::

## [Parliamentary Review]{.HTitleText1} {#h-142273 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Permanent review by Parliamentary
Committee

- **[[168]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-168 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  administration of this Act shall be reviewed on a permanent basis by
  such committee of the Senate, of the House of Commons or of both
  Houses of Parliament as may be designated or established for that
  purpose.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Review and report after five years

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The committee designated or established for the
  purpose of subsection (1) shall, within five years after the coming
  into force of this Act, undertake a comprehensive review of the
  provisions and operation of this Act, and shall, within a reasonable
  period thereafter, cause to be laid before each House of Parliament a
  report thereon.

## [Transitional]{.HTitleText1} {#h-142279 .Part}

Definition of [[former Act]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

- **[[169]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-169 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In
  this section, [[former Act]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the [Customs
  Act](/eng/acts/C-52.6), chapter C-40 of the Revised Statutes of
  Canada, 1970.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Pending proceedings under former Act

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Any proceedings instituted under the former Act
  before the commencement of this Act shall be continued and completed
  as if this Act and any regulations made hereunder had not been
  enacted.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Amounts owing under former Act

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Sections 143 to 147 apply in respect of any amount
  owing to Her Majesty in right of Canada under the former Act or any
  regulations made thereunder unless legal proceedings have been
  instituted under section 102 of the former Act in respect thereof.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Goods detained under former Act

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Section 102 applies in respect of goods detained
  under subsection 22(2) of the former Act if such goods are in the
  custody of an officer at the time this Act comes into force.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}CUFTA 2017

**[[169.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-169.1 .sectionLabel}** The provisions of
the [Customs Tariff](/eng/acts/C-54.011) and its regulations in respect
of goods that are imported from or exported to Ukraine, as they read
before the day on which this section comes into force, apply in respect
of any proceedings in relation to CUFTA 2017.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2024, c. 3, s. 22](#1448857-1486117){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1448857-1486117.html"}
:::

**[[Footnote

.sectionLabel}** This Act or any provision thereof shall come into force
on a day or days to be fixed by proclamation.

- --- 2002, c. 22, ss. 305 to 308

  - ###### Meaning of [[implementation date]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} {#meaning-of-implementation-date .MarginalNote}

    [305]{.lawlabel} In sections 306 to 320, [[implementation
    date]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the day on which Parts 3 and 4 come
    into force.

- --- 2002, c. 22, ss. 305 to 308

  - ###### Transitional treatment of duties on packaged spirits {#transitional-treatment-of-duties-on-packaged-spirits .MarginalNote}

    [306]{.lawlabel} The following rules apply to packaged spirits on
    which a duty, at a rate determined by the application of section 1
    of Part I of the schedule to the [Excise Act](/eng/acts/E-14), was
    imposed under that Act or levied under the [Customs
    Tariff](/eng/acts/C-54.011) but that had not become payable before
    the implementation date:

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} as of that day, the duty is relieved;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} as of that day, the [Excise Act](/eng/acts/E-14)
      ceases to apply in respect of the spirits;

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in the case of imported packaged spirits that
      have not been released under the [Customs Act](/eng/acts/C-52.6),
      this Act, the [Customs Act](/eng/acts/C-52.6) and the [Customs
      Tariff](/eng/acts/C-54.011) apply in respect of them as though
      they were imported on that day; and

    - [(d)]{.lawlabel} in the case of any other packaged spirits, this
      Act applies in respect of them as though

      - [(i)]{.lawlabel} they were produced and packaged in Canada on
        that day by the person having possession of them immediately
        before that day and the person were permitted under this Act to
        produce and package them, and

      - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} if the spirits are in the possession of a duty
        free shop or an accredited representative or delivered as ships'
        stores in accordance with the [Ships' Stores
        Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-96-40), they had been entered
        into an excise warehouse and then removed from the warehouse in
        accordance with paragraph 147(1)(a) on that day.

- --- 2002, c. 22, ss. 305 to 308

  - ###### Transitional treatment of duties on bulk spirits {#transitional-treatment-of-duties-on-bulk-spirits .MarginalNote}

    - [307]{.lawlabel} [(1)]{.lawlabel} The following rules apply to
      bulk spirits on which a duty, at a rate determined by the
      application of section 1 of Part I of the schedule to the [Excise
      Act](/eng/acts/E-14), was imposed under that Act or levied under
      the [Customs Tariff](/eng/acts/C-54.011) but that had not become
      payable before the implementation date:

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} as of that day, the duty is relieved;

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} as of that day, the [Excise
        Act](/eng/acts/E-14) ceases to apply in respect of the spirits;

      - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in the case of imported bulk spirits that have
        not been released under the [Customs Act](/eng/acts/C-52.6),
        this Act, the [Customs Act](/eng/acts/C-52.6) and the [Customs
        Tariff](/eng/acts/C-54.011) apply in respect of them as though
        they were imported on that day; and

      - [(d)]{.lawlabel} in the case of any other bulk spirits, this Act
        applies in respect of them as though they were produced in
        Canada on that day by the person having possession of them
        immediately before that day.

    - ###### Transitional treatment of bulk spirits imported for bottling or blending {#transitional-treatment-of-bulk-spirits-imported-for-bottling-or-blending .MarginalNote}

      [(2)]{.lawlabel} The following rules apply to bulk spirits on
      which a duty, at a rate determined by the application of section 1
      of Part I of the schedule to the [Excise Act](/eng/acts/E-14), was
      levied under the [Customs Tariff](/eng/acts/C-54.011) and remitted
      under the [Distilled Spirits for Bottling in Bond Remission
      Order](/eng/regulations/SOR-97-321) or the [Imported Spirits for
      Blending Remission Order](/eng/regulations/SI-83-151) before the
      implementation date:

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} as of that day, the duty imposed on the spirits
        under subsection 135(1) of the [Excise Act](/eng/acts/E-14) when
        they were entered into a distillery is relieved;

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} as of that day, the [Excise
        Act](/eng/acts/E-14) ceases to apply in respect of the spirits;
        and

      - [(c)]{.lawlabel} this Act applies in respect of them as though
        they were produced in Canada on that day by the person having
        possession of them immediately before that day.

- --- 2002, c. 22, ss. 305 to 308

  - ###### Transitional treatment of excise taxes on wine {#transitional-treatment-of-excise-taxes-on-wine .MarginalNote}

    [308]{.lawlabel} The following rules apply to wine on which tax was
    imposed under section 27 of the [Excise Tax Act](/eng/acts/E-15) but
    had not become payable before the implementation date:

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} as of that day, the tax is relieved;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} as of that day, Parts III, VI and VII of the
      [Excise Tax Act](/eng/acts/E-15) cease to apply in respect of the
      wine;

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in the case of imported wine that has not been
      released under the [Customs Act](/eng/acts/C-52.6), this Act, the
      [Customs Act](/eng/acts/C-52.6) and the [Customs
      Tariff](/eng/acts/C-54.011) apply in respect of the wine as though
      it were imported on that day;

    - [(d)]{.lawlabel} in the case of bulk wine to which paragraph (c)
      does not apply, this Act applies in respect of it as though it
      were produced in Canada on that day

      - [(i)]{.lawlabel} if the wine is located in a ferment-on-premises
        facility or at the residence of an individual, by the individual
        who owned the wine immediately before that day, or

      - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} in any other case, by the person having
        possession of it immediately before that day; and

    - [(e)]{.lawlabel} in the case of wine to which neither
      paragraph (c) nor (d) apply, this Act applies in respect of it as
      though

      - [(i)]{.lawlabel} it were produced and packaged in Canada on that
        day by the person having possession of it immediately before
        that day and the person were permitted under this Act to produce
        and package it, and

      - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} in the case of wine in the possession of a
        duty free shop or an accredited representative or delivered as
        ships' stores in accordance with the [Ships' Stores
        Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-96-40), it had been entered
        into an excise warehouse and then removed from the warehouse in
        accordance with paragraph 147(1)(a) on that day.

- --- 2002, c. 22, s. 317

  - ###### Transitional treatment of imported tobacco products {#transitional-treatment-of-imported-tobacco-products .MarginalNote}

    [317]{.lawlabel} The following rules apply to an imported tobacco
    product:

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if duty levied under section 21 of the [Customs
      Tariff](/eng/acts/C-54.011) and tax imposed under section 23 of
      the [Excise Tax Act](/eng/acts/E-15) on the product had not become
      payable before the implementation date,

      - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the duty and tax are relieved, and

      - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} this Act and the [Customs
        Act](/eng/acts/C-52.6) apply in respect of the product as though
        it were imported into Canada on that day by the importer;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the product was stamped or marked under the
      [Excise Act](/eng/acts/E-14), the product is deemed to be stamped
      or marked, as the case may be, under this Act; and

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the [Excise Act](/eng/acts/E-14) and Parts III,
      VI and VII of the [Excise Tax Act](/eng/acts/E-15) cease to apply
      in respect of the product.

- --- 2010, c. 12, s. 54

  - ###### Meaning of [[implementation date]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} {#meaning-of-implementation-date-1 .MarginalNote}

    - [54]{.lawlabel} [(1)]{.lawlabel} In this section, [[implementation
      date]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the first day of the month that
      follows the thirtieth day after the day on which this Act receives
      Royal Assent.

    - ###### Application {#application .MarginalNote}

      [(2)]{.lawlabel} Sections 38 to 46, subsections 47(1) and (3) and
      sections 48 to 53 apply as of the implementation date, except
      that, for the purposes of section 34 or 35 of the [Excise Act,
      2001](/eng/acts/E-14.1), a tobacco product may, on or after the
      implementation date but before April 2011, be entered into the
      duty-paid market or be released under the [Customs
      Act](/eng/acts/C-52.6) for entry into the duty-paid market, as the
      case may be, if it is stamped in one of the following manners:

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in accordance with the rules applicable under
        the [Excise Act, 2001](/eng/acts/E-14.1) as those rules read on
        the day before the day on which this Act receives Royal Assent;

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in accordance with the rules applicable under
        the [Excise Act, 2001](/eng/acts/E-14.1) as those rules read on
        the implementation date or as they are amended from time to time
        after that date; or

      - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in the manner described in paragraphs (a) and
        (b).

    - ###### Effect --- paragraph (2)(a) {#effect-paragraph-2a .MarginalNote}

      [(3)]{.lawlabel} If a tobacco product is stamped in the manner
      described in paragraph (2)(a), the rules applicable under the
      [Excise Act, 2001](/eng/acts/E-14.1) as those rules read on the
      day before the day on which this Act receives Royal Assent apply
      in respect of the tobacco product.

    - ###### Effect --- paragraph (2)(b) or (c) {#effect-paragraph-2b-or-c .MarginalNote}

      [(4)]{.lawlabel} If a tobacco product is stamped in the manner
      described in paragraph (2)(b) or (c), the rules applicable under
      the [Excise Act, 2001](/eng/acts/E-14.1) as those rules read on
      the implementation date, or as they are amended from time to time
      after that date, apply in respect of the tobacco product.

- --- 2020, c. 1, s. 119

  - ###### [2014, c. 14, s. 24]{.HistoricalNote} {#c.-14-s.-24 .MarginalNote}

    [119]{.lawlabel} Subsections 42.3(1) to (4) of the Act are replaced
    by the following:

    :::: section
    ::: AmendedText
    - ###### Definition of [[customs administration]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} {#definition-of-customs-administration .MarginalNote}

      - [42.3]{.lawlabel} [(1)]{.lawlabel} In this section, [[customs
        administration]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the meaning assigned to
        that expression by Article E-14 of CCFTA, Article V.14 of CCRFTA
        or Article 5.1 of CHFTA, as the case may be.

      - ###### Effective date of redetermination or further redetermination of origin of goods {#effective-date-of-redetermination-or-further-redetermination-of-origin-of-goods .MarginalNote}

        [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (4), a redetermination or
        further redetermination of origin does not take effect until
        notice of it is given to the importer of the goods and any
        person who completed and signed a Certificate of Origin for the
        goods if the result of the redetermination or further
        redetermination of origin made under subsection 59(1) in respect
        of goods for which preferential tariff treatment under CCFTA,
        CCRFTA or CHFTA is claimed and that are the subject of a
        verification of origin under this Act is that

        - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the goods are not eligible for that
          preferential tariff treatment on the basis of the tariff
          classification or value of one or more materials used in their
          production; and

        - [(b)]{.lawlabel} that tariff classification or value differs
          from the tariff classification or value applied to those
          materials by the country --- Chile, Costa Rica or Honduras ---
          from which the goods were exported.

      - ###### Limitation {#limitation .MarginalNote}

        [(3)]{.lawlabel} A redetermination or further redetermination of
        origin referred to in subsection (2) shall not be applied to
        goods imported before the date on which the notice was given if
        the customs administration of the country --- Chile, Costa Rica
        or Honduras --- from which the goods were exported, has, before
        that date,

        - [(a)]{.lawlabel} given an advance ruling under Article E-09 of
          CCFTA, Article V.9 of CCRFTA, paragraph 1 of Article 5.10 or
          paragraph 11 of Article 6.2 of CHFTA as the case may be, or
          given another ruling referred to in paragraph 12 of Article
          E-06 of CCFTA, paragraph 15 of Article V.6 of CCRFTA, or
          paragraph 15 of Article 5.7 of CHFTA, as the case may be, on
          the tariff classification or value of the materials referred
          to in subsection (2); or

        - [(b)]{.lawlabel} given consistent treatment with respect to
          the tariff classification or value of the materials referred
          to in subsection (2) on their importation into Chile, Costa
          Rica or Honduras.

      - ###### Postponement of effective date {#postponement-of-effective-date .MarginalNote}

        [(4)]{.lawlabel} The date on which a redetermination or further
        redetermination of origin referred to in subsection (2) takes
        effect shall be postponed for a period not exceeding 90 days if
        the importer of the goods that are the subject of the
        redetermination or further redetermination or any person who
        completed and signed a Certificate of Origin for the goods
        establishes to the satisfaction of the Minister that the
        importer or the person, as the case may be, has relied in good
        faith, to the detriment of the importer or person, on the tariff
        classification or value applied to the materials referred to in
        that subsection by the customs administration of the country ---
        Chile, Costa Rica or of Honduras --- from which the goods were
        exported.
    :::
    ::::

    :::: section

    - ###### Penalty or ascertained forfeiture {#penalty-or-ascertained-forfeiture .MarginalNote}

      [(2)]{.lawlabel} Any amount of money demanded as a penalty in a

      [(3)]{.lawlabel} Whenever the importer of the goods that have been
      released or any person authorized under paragraph 32(6)(a) or
      subsection 32(7) to account for goods becomes liable under this
      Act to pay duties on those goods, the owner of the goods at the

- --- 2023, c. 26, s. 476

  - - [476]{.lawlabel} [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subsections 11(1) to (3) of the
      Act are replaced by the following:

      :::: section
      ::: AmendedText
      - ###### Presentation on arrival in Canada {#presentation-on-arrival-in-canada .MarginalNote}

        - [11]{.lawlabel} [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to this section,
          every person arriving in Canada shall, except in the
          circumstances and subject to the conditions that may be
          prescribed, enter Canada only at a customs office designated
          for that purpose that is open for business and without delay
          present themselves

          - [(a)]{.lawlabel} to an officer in person; or

          - [(b)]{.lawlabel} to the Agency by a means of
            telecommunication that is specified by the Minister for use
            at that customs office.

        - ###### Limitation {#limitation-1 .MarginalNote}

          [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} If only one of the manners of presentation
          set out in paragraphs (1)(a) and (b) is made available at the
          customs office, the person shall present themselves under
          subsection (1) in that manner.

        - ###### Information {#information .MarginalNote}

          [(1.2)]{.lawlabel} The person presenting themselves under
          subsection (1) shall

          - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if presenting in person, provide any
            information that may be required by the officer in the
            performance of the officer's duties under this or any other
            Act of Parliament;

          - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if presenting by a means of
            telecommunication, provide any information, including a
            photograph taken when they are presenting themselves, that
            may be required by the Agency and that is related to the
            performance of an officer's duties under this or any other
            Act of Parliament; and

          - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in either case, answer truthfully any
            questions that are asked by an officer in the performance of
            those duties.

        - ###### Information before arrival {#information-before-arrival .MarginalNote}

          [(1.3)]{.lawlabel} A person who intends to present themselves
          by a means of telecommunication under paragraph (1)(b) shall,
          in the prescribed circumstances, provide the prescribed
          information within the prescribed time before they arrive in
          Canada.

        - ###### Exception {#exception .MarginalNote}

          [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply to any person
          who has presented themselves outside Canada at a customs
          office designated for that purpose and has not subsequently
          stopped at any other place prior to their arrival in Canada,
          unless an officer requires them to comply with that
          subsection.

        - ###### Presentation of passengers and crew {#presentation-of-passengers-and-crew .MarginalNote}

          [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subject to this section, every person in
          charge of a conveyance arriving in Canada shall, except in the
          circumstances and subject to the conditions that may be
          prescribed, ensure that the passengers and crew are
          immediately on arrival in Canada transported to a customs
          office referred to in subsection (1).
      :::
      ::::

    - [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection 11(7) of the Act is replaced by the
      following:

      :::: section
      ::: AmendedText
      - ###### Powers of officer {#powers-of-officer .MarginalNote}

        [(7)]{.lawlabel} An officer may require that a person present
        themselves in person in accordance with paragraph (1)(a) at a
        time and place that the officer may specify, even if the person

        - [(a)]{.lawlabel} presents themselves, or expresses an
          intention to present themselves, at a customs office by a
          means of telecommunication under paragraph (1)(b);

        - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is exempt in the prescribed circumstances
          referred to in subsection (1) from presenting themselves in
          accordance with that subsection;

        - [(c)]{.lawlabel} holds an authorization under subsection
          11.1(1); or

        - [(d)]{.lawlabel} is authorized under the regulations made
          under subsection 11.1(3).

      - ###### Regulations {#regulations .MarginalNote}

        [(8)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may make regulations
        respecting the establishment of classes of persons for the
        purposes of presentation under this section, including
        regulations respecting

        - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the issuance, amendment, suspension, renewal,
          cancellation and reinstatement by the Minister of
          authorizations for membership in a class;

        - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the requirements and conditions that are to
          be met before the authorizations may be issued;

        - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the terms and conditions of the
          authorizations; and

        - [(d)]{.lawlabel} fees or the manner of determining fees to be
          paid for the authorizations.

      - ###### [Service Fees Act](/eng/acts/S-8.4) {#service-fees-act .MarginalNote}

        [(9)]{.lawlabel} The [Service Fees Act](/eng/acts/S-8.4) does
        not apply to a fee for an authorization issued under regulations
        made under subsection (8) if it is a reciprocal fee under an
        international arrangement.
      :::
      ::::

- --- 2023, c. 26, s. 477

  - [477]{.lawlabel} Section 11.7 of the Act is replaced by the
    following:

    :::: section
    ::: AmendedText
    - ###### Person travelling in mixed-traffic corridor {#person-travelling-in-mixed-traffic-corridor .MarginalNote}

      - [11.7]{.lawlabel} [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every person who is
        travelling in a mixed-traffic corridor shall, at the nearest
        customs office,

        - [(a)]{.lawlabel} present themselves

          - [(i)]{.lawlabel} to an officer in person, or

          - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} to the Agency by the means of
            telecommunication that is specified by the Minister for use
            at the customs office; and

        - [(b)]{.lawlabel} state whether they are arriving from a
          location outside or within Canada.

      - ###### Limitation {#limitation-2 .MarginalNote}

        [(2)]{.lawlabel} If only one of the manners of presentation set
        out in subparagraphs (1)(a)(i) and (ii) is made available at the
        customs office, the person shall present themselves under
        paragraph (1)(a) in that manner.
    :::
    ::::

- --- 2023, c. 26, s. 478

  - [478]{.lawlabel} The Act is amended by adding the following after
    section 12.1:

    :::: section
    ::: AmendedText
    - ###### Commercial passenger aircraft --- baggage {#commercial-passenger-aircraft-baggage .MarginalNote}

      - [12.2]{.lawlabel} [(1)]{.lawlabel} Despite section 14 but
        subject to the regulations, every operator of an aircraft that
        is arriving in Canada and carrying passengers who have paid for
        passage shall ensure that the baggage of all passengers and crew
        --- other than baggage in the passengers' or crew's actual
        possession --- is, without delay on arrival, transported to the
        nearest designated international baggage area.

      - ###### Powers of officer {#powers-of-officer-1 .MarginalNote}

        [(2)]{.lawlabel} An officer may require that an operator who is
        exempted from the requirement set out in subsection (1) ensure
        that the baggage be brought without delay to the location
        specified by the officer.

      - ###### Regulations {#regulations-1 .MarginalNote}

        [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may make regulations
        respecting exemptions from the requirement set out in subsection
        (1).

      - ###### Designation {#designation .MarginalNote}

        [(4)]{.lawlabel} The President may designate an area in an
        airport as an international baggage area for the purposes of
        subsection (1).

      - ###### Amendment, etc., of designation {#amendment-etc.-of-designation .MarginalNote}

        [(5)]{.lawlabel} The President may at any time amend, cancel or
        reinstate a designation made under subsection (4).
    :::
    ::::
